   - Neighbors of E: A, B, F, S
   - Neighbors of J: H
   - Neighbors of Q: G, I, O, R
   - Neighbors of O: C, G, I, Q, R
   - Neighbors of P: C, R

3. **Next level of neighbors:**
   - From B's neighbors: None of A, E, F, S, T are connected to R directly or indirectly. For E's neighbors too: none are connected to R.
   - From Q's neighbors: G, I are not connected to H directly or indirectly.
   - For O's neighbors: C, G, I are connected to B, F, N respectively, but none provide a path to H except G -> B -> H. However, this path is longer.
   - For P's neighbors: C -> F -> S -> T -> B -> H which is longer. Direct connect not found.

Upon examining possible paths:
   - Path from H through B, S, T, G, and then through Q to R seems longer.
   - Similarly other connections were traversed but they all had more edges.

Upon identification of a more feasible path through explicit iteration from B's interconnections and conclusions:

     H -> B -> S -> T -> G -> Q -> R

   - Direct neighbors of T: B, G, H.

   - From B, the shortest route to T is directly through one edge.
   - Therefore, the path S -> B -> T has a length of 2.

3. **Evaluate Other Potential Paths:**

   - From S to F and then to B (S -> F -> B -> T), but S -> B is shorter.
   - From S to A and then to E or B is longer than S -> B directly.
   - From T to G, H, or other connected nodes and then back to S or another intermediate node represents longer paths due to additional edges.

Thus, the shortest path is through B, which connects the two black nodes with a length of 2.
